Sam took the turn and shot up the path. He fell, and 
if the reeds were short, he got up and followed, running. He reached the edge of the 
grassy Parth. Galen reached the bank, where the boats were 
bags of water. Nobody had anything there. There seemed 
to be grit in the woods, but don't pay them much attention. 
If you look at it for a moment, it will stop and freeze. A 
single barrel slid down the bank. With a shout, Sam 
ran down the grass. The bottle slid into the water. 

Come on, Mr. Frodo! "Come on!" shouted Sam, and he ran 
from the bank, clutching the bottle that was drifting away. I was off 
by a meter. With a grit and a splash, he fell flat on his face 
into deep, fast-flowing water. Gurgling, it sank, and the river 
looked over my curly head. 

A gasp of dismay came from the empty boat. The 
oar stopped, and the boat turned. Frodo arrived just in 
time to grab Sam by the hair as he lay submerged, bubbling and 
struggling. My heart shone in my dark brown eyes. " 

Up you go, Sam, lad!" said Frodo. "Now get back to my 
hand! 

" "Save me, Mr. Frodo!" gasped Sam. "I wonder. There's no way 
to reach your hand." 

"Here it is. Don't pinch me, lad! Don't stand alone. Keep 
a float and don't touch, or you'll tip the bottle over. 
Now, hold on to the rib and let me use the oar!" 

With a few strokes, Frodo pulled the bottle from the 
bank, and Sam was able to climb aboard, wet as a puddle. 
Frodo laid down the ring and returned to the bowl. "
